Supplemental Reader-LM evaluation

PARTIAL RUN — NOT a full-run comparison or ranking

Supplemental add-on benchmark conducted AFTER the original heldout results were known. This is NOT a prospective blind selection experiment or a fresh holdout. Reader-LM uses a constrained input/output model run; truncation and output caps can affect anchor retention. The report makes no endpoint requests and loads no external assets.

Ground truth is SOURCE-ONLY AI-assisted selected anchors, NOT human gold or whole-document precision/recall. Exact substring presence does not assess factual fidelity, usability, or structural relationships. Human acceptance gates are NOT ASSESSED; no threshold achievement is certified. This purposive sample does not establish population accuracy. No uncertainty interval is claimed.

Missing HTML outcomes remain zero-retention failures under the frozen scoring rules. Missing non-HTML outcomes remain visible in the ledger. Partial runs must not be ranked against completed methods. Complete means recorded outcomes, not successful extraction.

Scores use canonical plain text from the runner’s Markdown-to-block conversion. Generated output is not assumed to map faithfully to raw source positions. Control-header removal and finish reasons are recorded in per-sample diagnostics. Token/resource totals cover recorded supported attempts only; unknown values are not zero.

CDN Network Providers: Unraveling the Intricacies of Latency Maps and PoP Counts

Amidst today's digital revolution, where milliseconds can decide the success of an online transaction and smooth streaming experiences are sacred for global audiences, the art and science behind Content Delivery Networks (CDNs) have never been more critical. Exploring the interplay between latency maps and Points of Presence (PoPs) not only provides insight into raw performance numbers but also sheds light on strategic infrastructure investments made by leading CDN network providers. In this in-depth analysis, we demystify how latency assessments and PoP counts contribute to the overall efficiency of a CDN, and why these metrics can have a profound impact across various industries ranging from media to gaming and software.

Rather than starting with generic statements, picture your user base spread out over continents, facing varied network conditions—each millisecond's delay translates to a potential loss in revenue or engagement. Your customers demand real-time interaction and rapid content delivery, and the decision of which CDN partner to entrust with your digital assets may mean the difference between virality and stagnation. This article unpacks all these nuances through detailed comparisons, real-life performance data, and analyses driven by the very latest research from industry thought leaders and the top articles published in 2025.

Understanding the CDN Landscape: Fundamentals of Latency and PoP Distribution

To truly appreciate the network behind content delivery, one must first grasp the underlying building blocks. In the realm of CDNs, two metrics stand out as indispensable indicators of performance: latency and PoP counts. Latency, often the silent killer of web performance, measures the delay between a request and the delivery of content. While lower latency inherently means faster website load times and a smoother user experience, achieving consistently low latency across diverse geographies is an ongoing challenge for CDN providers.

On the other hand, PoP counts represent the strategic geographical deployment of servers that cache content closer to the end user. Each PoP acts as a localized mini-hub, reducing the distance data must travel. The sheer number of PoPs, along with their distribution across key markets, correlates strongly with a CDN’s ability to maintain low latency under heavy load. Today’s providers are frequently judged on how well they manage these two parameters—latency maps offer a visually engaging narrative of network performance, while PoP counts provide the quantitative backup. In this context, making an informed decision translates to choosing the CDN that best aligns with your target market's geography and performance expectations.

Techniques for Mapping Latency and Interpreting Data

As technology evolves, so do the methodologies for measuring latency. Whether for streaming high-definition video or ensuring real-time data in financial applications, modern latency mapping tools have become remarkably sophisticated. Tools such as traceroutes, synthetic benchmarking, and real-user monitoring (RUM) are routinely employed to create detailed latency maps that reflect real-world scenarios. Academic studies, including research published by organizations like the Internet Engineering Task Force (IETF), have underlined the importance of these tools in driving improvements in CDN performance.

Latency maps typically display information such as average response times, jitter, packet loss, and bandwidth utilization across multiple regions. When interpreting these maps, several key indicators should be considered:

Geographical Hotspots: Areas with consistently high latency indicate potential infrastructural bottlenecks or insufficient PoP coverage.

Temporal Fluctuations: Observing how latency metrics change over time can highlight peak usage periods or emerging issues during network congestion.

Path Efficiency: Evaluating the routes data takes from the origin server to the end user can reveal inefficiencies and opportunities for route optimization.

The incorporation of these sophisticated analytical tools into CDN performance dashboards is now common practice among leading providers. The comparative analysis of latency maps not only highlights the strengths and weaknesses of each provider but also informs decision-makers of the trade-offs between cost, performance, and geographical coverage.

Decoding PoP Counts: More Than Just Numbers

At its simplest, a PoP is a data center strategically positioned closer to digital consumers. However, a higher PoP count does not automatically equate to superior performance. Factors such as hardware quality, network interconnectivity, and even local regulations can influence the effectiveness of these nodes. A provider with a smaller but more strategically placed network of PoPs might outperform a competitor boasting a higher infrastructure density in regions that matter most.

Recent industry reports, such as those by BlazingCDN, highlight how providers optimize PoP deployment by focusing on areas with the highest internet traffic and demand. In many ways, PoP counts serve as a proxy for the targeted investment in regional infrastructure. Beyond mere quantity, the quality of a PoP is measured by:

Network Interconnectivity: How well a PoP connects with local Internet Service Providers (ISPs) and transit networks.

Scalability: The capacity of a PoP to handle surges in traffic, particularly during peak periods or high-demand events.

Redundancy: The availability of backup systems and failover mechanisms that maintain service continuity.

When comparing PoP strategies, it is critical to analyze both the raw numbers and the qualitative aspects of these nodes. Quality factors play a pivotal role in determining the actual end-user experience, especially for applications that require real-time data delivery, such as live sports streaming, online gaming, or financial trading platforms.

Comparative Analysis of Leading CDN Providers

The competitive landscape of CDNs is as dynamic as it is expansive. Some of the most established providers have refined their networks over decades, while emerging companies are pushing the envelope on innovation and cost efficiency. In our analysis, we looked at key performance metrics such as latency, PoP numbers, cost per GB, and technological adaptability. The top three articles from 2025 have unanimously emphasized that the combination of broad geographical PoP distribution and consistently low latency metrics is paramount for digital success.

This table, though simplified, encapsulates not only the quantitative aspects of each provider’s network but also hints at the qualitative aspects of service. For instance, BlazingCDN stands out by offering lower costs and competitive latency metrics—attributes that are especially beneficial for industries with tight margins and high performance requirements.

Latency: The Invisible Force Shaping User Experience

In the world of digital interaction, latency remains one of the most potent influencers of user experience. The difference between 30 ms and 60 ms might seem negligible on paper, but in applications such as multiplayer gaming or live auctions, these milliseconds can tilt the scales significantly. User expectations have soared in tandem with advancements in technology, and a CDN’s ability to ensure low latency is often the measure of its reliability and modernity.

To dig deeper, it is vital to understand that latency is not a static measure. In practice, latency is influenced by several factors:

Network Congestion: Traffic volume fluctuations can lead to temporary slowdowns, hence dynamic latency monitoring is crucial.

Routing Efficiency: The path data takes from source to destination can significantly impact transmission times.

Server Load: How many simultaneous connections a server manages can affect response time.

Advanced latency maps produced by modern CDNs illustrate these trends in visually articulable formats, often using heat maps that highlight areas with optimal versus suboptimal performance. For instance, during peak hours in Asia, some providers might show slight degradation in performance compared to off-peak times—a critical insight for companies wanting to optimize resource allocation and customer experience.

Real-World Performance: Bridging the Gap Between Numbers and Experience

It is one thing to analyze data points on a map and another to see how they translate into user experiences. Consider online gaming, where any hint of latency can render gameplay unsatisfactory. High PoP counts in regions dense with gamers can substantially cut down delay times and provide a smoother overall experience. Similarly, for media companies broadcasting live events, ensuring minimal buffering is paramount—therefore, selecting a CDN with stellar latency performances in their target demographics is essential.

Industry benchmarks suggest that networks with an average latency below 40 ms often rank highly in user satisfaction surveys. Moreover, studies such as those from the Digital Media Performance Report underscore the correlation between low latency and improved viewer engagement and retention rates. By integrating these insights into operational strategies, companies in the media, gaming, and financial services sectors can significantly enhance their competitive advantage.

Examining PoP Counts: Quantitative Insights and Strategic Implications

While latency provides a measure of speed, PoP counts give a picture of a CDN provider's reach. However, as emphasized earlier, a higher PoP count is only as good as the deployment quality. A deep dive into PoP strategies reveals that providers with fewer, high-quality PoPs can often outperform those with a larger but less efficient network. Strategic PoP placement ensures that data is cached as close to consumers as possible, which reduces the physical distances data travels and thus minimizes potential delays.

Let’s explore some pivotal aspects:

Regional Distribution: CDNs must carefully map out where their major user bases reside and install PoPs in those regions. For example, while North America and Europe might boast several high-capacity nodes, emerging markets in Asia and South America represent growth opportunities that require tailored solutions.

Infrastructure Investment: The sustainability and scalability of a PoP network directly affect how well providers

VA Loans Expertise

As a Top VA lender,1 we at Navy Federal understand the unique needs of servicemembers, Veterans and their families, and can help you take advantage of the exclusive VA loan benefits you've earned. We’re here to help you through the home-buying experience by providing low VA home loan rates, resources and membership benefits.

VA Loans Offer Many Benefits. They're an excellent option if you qualify and are looking to buy a home.

Image 1

Options With No Down Payment

You can buy a house now without waiting years to save with no-down-payment options.Footnote 4 Check your certificate of eligibility to start.

Image 2

No Private Mortgage Insurance Required

VA loans don't require Private Mortgage Insurance (PMI).

Image 3

Seller Contribution of Up to 4%

The seller is allowed to contribute up to 4% of the value of the home toward your qualifying closing costs, which could save you money.

Image 4

Refinance Options Available

Ready to apply? Sign in to get started.

Apply Now for a VA Home Loan

Not a member? Join today.

Our U.S.-based member service reps are here for you 24/7. Reach us online, over the phone or at a branch. Contact us. If you're ready to apply for a mortgage or want to check the status of your loan, please call us at 1-888-842-6328.

Visit Our Home Buying Center

Our Home Buying Center has everything you need to de-stress your next address. Learn about your loan options, the benefits of a preapproval letter and different ways you could save money.

Learn More

Find a Real Estate AgentFootnote 5

Finding the right home starts with finding the right real estate agent. Use RealtyPlus® to connect with a top-performing real estate agent in your area. Plus, you could earn cash back. Exclusively for Navy Federal members.Footnote 6

Learn More about RealtyPlus.

Discount points are included. Occupancy restriction applies. All loans subject to credit approval.

1

ElevenLabs

Funding: $351M ElevenLabs is a research company developing AI voice synthesis software for creators and publishers.

2

Image 2

Funding: $10M WellSaid Labs has developed the art text-to-speech technology that creates life-like synthetic voice, from the voices of real people.

3

Image 3

Funding: $6M Voice AI is a real-time speech-to-speech AI voice changing platform and UGC content platform.

4

Dubformer

Image 4

Funding: $3.6M Dubformer is an advanced AI-driven video dubbing solution designed for media and entertainment localization, offering high-quality dubbing and voice-over services with a blend of cutting-edge AI and human quality control.

5

AMAI

Image 5

Funding: $600K AMAI develops ultra-realistic text-to-speech engine

6

AppTek

Image 6

AppTek combines cutting-edge artificial intelligence research with meaningful and transformative real-world applications. Our team consists of world-leading scientists with an extensive list of patents, innovations and academic publications contributing to the advancement of neural network and machine learning science and technology. Based on our scientific research, our engineering team helps convert these advancements into commercially viable real-world applications that improve the daily life in areas including accessibility, commerce, trade, and communication across languages.

Il est enfin disponible ! L'Arcep a dévoilé son Observatoire annuel sur la qualité des services mobiles en métropole, et l'identité de ce dauphin des télécoms n'en finit pas de surprendre. Image 1: Floriane Wittner

Par Floriane Wittner

Rédactrice actualités télécoms

Publié le 06/07/2025

3 min de lecture

Il est enfin disponible ! L'Arcep a dévoilé son Observatoire annuel sur la qualité des services mobiles en métropole, et l'identité de ce dauphin des télécoms n'en finit pas de surprendre. Pourtant, cette distinction est méritée à plus d'un titre. Le deuxième meilleur réseau mobile de France est disponible à partir de 5,99 € par mois. De quoi profiter de l'été à prix malin !

09 71 07 91 57

Des résultats concrets sur les usages mobiles

Image 2: service client sfr

L’enquête menée par l'Arcep porte comme toujours sur les appels voix, les SMS, l’internet mobile et même les appels voix passés via WhatsApp. Dans cette dernière catégorie, SFR se hisse premier ex æquo sur la qualité des appels !

Les débits de téléchargement et de téléversement pour SFR s’améliorent de 3 à 6 points par rapport à 2023, le plaçant en deuxième position. La navigation web est également très bonne, avec un temps de chargement de moins de 5 secondes.

Le streaming vidéo suit la même tendance, où SFR se classe deuxième ex æquo. C’est le reflet tangible de la qualité réseau fournie par l'opérateur au carré rouge.

Image 3: taux debits descendants sfr

Envie de souscrire une offre mobile SFR ? Découvrez les offres disponibles et laissez-vous guider afin de profiter de l'offre partenaire la plus adaptée à vos besoins.

09 71 07 91 57

SFR : un réseau performant dans les transports

Image 4: pouce selectra

Là où SFR parvient à véritablement se démarquer, c'est dans les transports publics. Et ça, c'est un excellent point pour l'opérateur, à l'heure où les utilisateurs se déplacent toujours plus vite, emportent avec eux smartphones, tablettes ou ordinateurs pour garder leurs contenus à portée de main et pouvoir communiquer en permanence.

L’Arcep mesure effectivement la performance réseau sur les axes routiers, en métros et dans les trains. SFR arrive ainsi premier ex æquo pour la navigation web dans les métros. Il se positionne également deuxième sur les axes routiers et les trains intercités/TER et TGV.

Concrètement, la stabilité des connexions et la fluidité des usages en déplacement sont largement satisfaisantes. Cela profite aux appels, à la navigation et au contenu multimédia.

En fait, ce qu'il faut retenir de ce nouvel Observatoire de la qualité des services mobiles en métropole, c'est que SFR délivre des performances désormais très proches de celles d'Orange, avec notamment un positionnement renforcé par des forfaits avantageux.

Note: Pricing and product information are correct as of May 3, 2024, and subject to change.

The virtual machine market is witnessing an uptrend, driven by the rapid growth of cloud computing technologies and the increasing demand for scalable computing resources. With a market size value of USD 11,277.6 million in 2023, the virtual machine market is projected to experience a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 15.1% from 2023 to 2030, reaching a revenue forecast of USD 30,122.3 million by 2030.

Within this thriving market, among many hyperscalers, Microsoft Azure offers a suite of cloud services, including Azure virtual machines (Azure VMs). As businesses increasingly adopt cloud resources and migrate their applications and data to the cloud, understanding virtual machine pricing is essential for effective cloud cost optimization and budgeting. Cloud costs can quickly escalate if not monitored and optimized correctly, leading to unexpected expenses and potential financial strain. This article digs into the details of Azure VM pricing, exploring pricing models, pricing factors, cost estimation tools, optimization strategies, and best practices.

Key takeaways:

Azure VM pricing uses complex structures with separate charges for compute, storage, networking, licensing, and various additional features across multiple VM families and payment models that make cost prediction extremely challenging.

DigitalOcean Droplets offer transparent, all-inclusive pricing starting at $4/month with compute, storage, and bandwidth included in single predictable prices with no hidden fees or separate charges for network traffic within reasonable limits.

Azure requires careful planning and monitoring to avoid unexpected costs from idle resources, egress fees, premium storage charges, and various add-on services, while DigitalOcean provides straightforward billing that simplifies budgeting and cost management.

Azure Virtual Machine pricing refers to the cost structure of deploying and running virtual machines (VMs) on Microsoft's Azure cloud platform. The pricing model for Azure VMs allows customers to choose from various VM types, VM series, sizes, and configurations to meet their specific compute capacity needs.

Microsoft Azure offers several pricing models for virtual machines, allowing organizations to optimize their cloud costs based on their specific workload requirements and budget considerations. Each pricing model caters to different use cases and budget requirements, allowing organizations to optimize their Azure virtual machine costs based on their specific needs. The primary pricing models include:

Pay-as-you-go pricing for Azure VMs

Part of the pay-as-you cloud computing model, Azure’s pay-as-you-go flexible pricing model lets customers pay for Azure virtual machine instances based on usage without any upfront commitments or long-term contracts. Pay-as-you-go pricing is ideal for production workloads with variable or unpredictable compute demands, providing the ability to scale resources up or down as needed.

Reserved instances

Azure offers reserved instances involving upfront payments for a one-year or three-year commitment. By reserving compute capacity in advance, customers can benefit from cost savings compared to pay-as-you-go pricing. Reserved instances are a cost-effective option for applications with consistent resource needs. This pricing model best suits customers with predictable workloads or steady-state computing requirements.

Azure savings plan for compute

The Azure savings plan is a pricing model for compute resource usage. Suppose the actual usage of Azure components (like virtual machines, Azure Databricks, and Azure Container Instances) is less than or equal to the hourly amount committed in the savings plan. In that case, organizations benefit from the lower, discounted savings plan prices for those compute resources.

However, if the usage exceeds the hourly amount committed in the savings plan, the pricing model adjusts accordingly. The usage portion up to the hourly amount is billed at the lower savings plan prices, which are included in the overall plan cost. Any additional usage beyond the hourly amount is then billed separately at the standard pay-as-you-go prices. This plan is particularly useful for businesses with dynamic or unpredictable workloads that require the flexibility to scale compute resources up or down without being tied to specific VM configurations.

Spot virtual machines

Azure spot virtual machines offer an opportunity to capitalize on unutilized Azure capacity as spot instances are offered at significantly reduced costs compared to pay-as-you-go pricing. These instances are ideal for batch processing, high-performance computing, and other workloads that can tolerate interruptions, as Azure can reclaim the resources at any time based on capacity availability.

Factors affecting Azure VM pricing

Azure virtual machine pricing is influenced by several key factors, which determine the overall cost of deploying and running virtual machines on the Microsoft Azure cloud platform.

Virtual machine size

The virtual machine size is a crucial factor directly impacting Azure VM pricing. The size encompasses the compute resources allocated to the virtual machine, including CPU, RAM, storage capacity, and high throughput networking capabilities. Generally, larger virtual machine instances with more powerful CPUs, higher RAM configurations, and enhanced networking performance command higher prices. Choosing the right VM size helps to ensure optimal performance while managing costs effectively.

Operating system

The choice of operating system plays a role in determining Azure VM pricing. Microsoft Azure supports both Windows server and Linux virtual machines, with pricing varying based on the selected operating system. Organizations may benefit from the Azure Hybrid Benefit for Windows server workloads, which allows them to use their existing Microsoft licenses.

Azure region

Microsoft's global data center infrastructure is divided into multiple Azure regions, and the cost of Azure services, including virtual machines, can vary across these regions. The pricing for Azure VMs is influenced by local infrastructure costs, regional demand, and data residency requirements. Organizations should carefully consider the Azure region to determine which best aligns with their operational needs, performance requirements, and cost optimization goals.

Azure VM type

Azure offers a diverse range of virtual machine types optimized for different workload scenarios. The pricing for these specialized VM types varies based on their configurations and capabilities, ensuring that customers pay for the resources aligned with their specific application and workload requirements. The following table gives a short description of different types of Azure VMs.

In-play Betting & Live Streaming

If you want to enjoy the most thrilling way to bet on sports, we recommend that you try live betting. You can wager on sports such as football and tennis as they unfold in real-time at MarathonBet Italy, with plenty of markets available for single, multi and system bets.

To get started, head to the Live tab where you will find an overview of the events that are currently in play. You can either make selections from this pge, or click on the events to find more markets for each game. One of the biggest advantages of MarathonBet is that there is live streaming available. This is a feature that allows you to watch games as you wager on them. Streams will usually include bet suggestions and graphic overlays so that you can quickly react to take advantage of the best prices.

Multiples Bets

MarathonBet provides users with a chance to make alternative bets to single bets. Multiple bets allow you to add several markets to one bet. When you click on the Multi tab within the bet slip, the odds of all the markets will be calculated together for you to place one stake on.

You can add up to 20 markets to your multi bet. However, to be successful, all of your selections must come through. This is why your odds to win will reduce with every new selection. We therefore recommend that you be more conservative with your picks for a multi, or make low-stake bets.

Another supported alternative to multis is the system bets. System bets are similar to multis in that you can make multiple market selections though you can still earn a payout if some legs don’t come through. You will find the available systems as you make your picks.

Bet and Win Limits at MarathonBet Italy

Like all major betting sites MarathonBet sets limits on how much you can win with a single bet. In any case, you are able to wager from as little as 1c. This is very accommodating to low-budget players. As for maximum bets, this will depend on whether the potential win will exceed the payout cap.

Single bet payouts are capped at €10,000 per bet. This is in accordance with Italian online gambling regulations and is the case at all bookmaker sites. For multiples and systems, winnings are capped at €10,000 per line and €50,000 in total for all winning lines. There are no daily win limits.

The average MarathonBet house edge is 4.5%, which is very reasonable. The house edge informs the odds at betting sites, and the lower it is, the higher that odds can be. Keep in mind that that is just the average for all sports, and some sports will actually have much smaller payout percentages.

The major sports tend to offer better payouts so that they can be more competitive, hence why football odds are usually so fair. This means that less popular markets will suffer to make up for any loss in profits. You can find the average payout variances according to MarathonBet sports below.

The live MarathonBet odds are less predictable as they will rapidly fluctuate. Regardless, we took a live odds comparison and discovered that the site offers fair prices for live games. Be aware that the data and statistics may have delays depending on the progress of games so odds may not always be accurate.

We suggest that you always do an odds comparison for pre-match betting markets. The best way to do this is to hold multiple bookmaker accounts so you can easily place your bets with the best priced bookie. You can also use the matched betting strategy to guarantee a profit from your welcome bonuses..
